Boats arriving from Tangyang pay full inward dues if discharing at Shasi town, local trade dues if discharging ear the West station; but in the Shipping table they are all ente as local traders. In the same way boats fron Shasi town to Tangyang pay full outward dues, but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang or other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table first as local entries, then as interport learances. Boata arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local clearances. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 0, 1908.
272 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-8EPT., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 454. SANTUAO DISTRICT. INCOG. ISLAND LIGHT ESTABLISHED. NoTCs is hereby given that Incog. Island Light was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 14th June. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, revolving, group-flashing, of the fourth order, showing double white flashes at intervals of 20 seconds. The lighthouse stands on the western extremity of the western island of the Incog. Group, and the light, which is elevated 149 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 18j nautical miles. The light is obscured to the south-westward by the small islet to the S.E. of Fuyan Island, on a bearing of approximately N. 51o E., but shows a small arc of about I between this islet and the main island. It is -also obscured on various bearings by the easterly islands of the Incog. Group between the bearings of about S. 711&#39; W. through west to about N. 40 W. All bearings are magnetic and from seaward. The tower is a steel structure, with a total height from base to lantern vane of 61 feet. The tower and dwellings are painted white. Approximate position:- Latitude... . . . . . . . . . 26 59&#39; 8&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . . . . . 120* 27&#39; 45&quot; E. SHANGHAI, 9th Julti i9o8.

NOTICE is hereby given that the following Signals, for indicating the depth of water on the Liao River Bar, are exhibited from a signal mast erected on the left bank of the river near Nodding Tommy Beacon:- A BALL IS HOISTED AT THE MASTHEAD WHEN THE TIDE IS RISING AND IS KEPT UP SO LCNG AS THE TIDE CONTINUES TO RISE. OCUSTOM[ GAZETTE. JULY-8EPT., 1908. No. 456. YANGTZE RIVER-SHANGiAI DISTRICT. CONFUCIUS CHANNEL. ALTERATION IN BuOYAGE. NOTIon is hereby given that the following changes in the positions of Buoys marking the Confucius Channel have been made:- South-east Spit Buoy (gas-lighted) is now moored in 36 feet at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, South-west Beacon bears N. 23&#39; E., distant 3.9 miles. Actcon Boy is now moored in 6o feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the bucy,&#39;Sotsh-west Beacon bears S. 83* E., distant 5.7 miles. All bearings given are magnetic. SAN., 24th August 1908. No. 457. ANGTZE RIVER-SHANGHAI DISTRICT. CONFUOIUS CHANNEL AND LANGSHAN OROSSING. ALTERATION Il4 BUOYAGE. NOTICE is hereby given that the following changes in the buoyage of the Confucius Channel and the Langshan Crossing have been made:- Centaur Lower Buoy is now moored in 40 feet at low water of spring tides. From the nuoy, Fork Tree Beacon bears N. 87&#39; W., distant 2.9 miles. Centaur Upp Buoy has been changed in colour from black to red and now marks the, northern side of the channel. It is moored in 38 feet at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Fork Tree Beacon bears S. 21&#39; W., distant 2.5 miles. Actxon Shoal Upper Buoy is now moored in 56 feet at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Plover Point Beaeon bears S. 82&#39; W., distant 4.4 miles. North Tree Rocks Buoy has been discontinued. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 28th September i9o8. 44
